2. RECOGNITION 
A.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This evening we 
are proud to recognize the accomplishments of ten  individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher Award Program. 
The nominees represent exemplary educators at every level and were nominated by 
their peers, by parents and by former and current students, all of whom talked 
about the impact these teachers have on the lives of those around them. Their 
nomination letters described how the professionals we honor here tonight help 
students find their voices, their passion and their love for learning.  
Would you please come forwa rd as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor twenty-four outstanding nominees, who have touched the lives of 
countless students and families in the Troy School District.  We are grateful for their 
dedication and passion for student success. 
Marianne Belleville, Kindergarten teacher, Wass Elementary School 
Karen Bush, 3rd Grade teacher, Schroeder Elementary School 
Lauri Bussell, Physical Education teacher, Martell Elementary 
       Christiansen, English Language Arts, Larson Middle School 
Jason Cichowicz, 5th Grade teacher, Schroeder Elementary School 
Genia Connell, 3rd Grade teacher, Leonard Elementary School 
      Costello, Math teacher, Baker Middle School 
Erin Detmer, 2nd Grade teacher, Leonard Elementary 
        Gibson, English Language Arts teacher, Smith Middle School 
Lora Herbert, 4th Grade teacher, Hill Elementary 
Stephanie Hyska, ELL Coordinator and French Teacher, Boulan Park Middle School 
Natalie Jagels, English Language Arts teacher, Smith Middle School 
Lindsay Keegan, 4th Grade teacher, Schroeder Elementary School 
       Maliepaard, 5th Grade teacher, Martell Elementary School 
      Martin, 5th Grade teacher, Morse Elementary 
      Martin, Math teacher, Boulan Park Middle School 
         McKinney, 4th Grade teacher, Costello Elementary School 
Trudi Motzenbecker, Reading Specialist, Wattles Elementary School 
        Pickell, Kindergarten teacher, Martell Elementary School 
Kitty Schmidt, Special Education teacher, Baker Middle School 
Amy Wallace, 4th Grade teacher, Leonard Elementary School 
3
Three of our district nominees were sent to Oakland Schools to represent Troy in 
the county -wide outstanding teacher award program. Due to the criteria set by 
Oakland Schools, Ann Lenihan, 2nd grade Teacher at Cos tello Elementary was 
nominated at the elementary level; Jennifer Taylor , Math Teacher from Boulan 
Park Middle School was nominated at the middle school level; and John Morrison, 
Physics Teacher from Troy High School was nominated at the high school level.  
Mrs. Lenihan  was also selected as the District’s overall Teacher of the Year and 
awarded a free one -year lease of a car, donated through the generosity of the 
Suburban Collection.  The three  candidates were chosen by a TEA selection 
committee to represent the District.   
At this time, I would like to invite Mrs. Lenihan, Ms. Taylor and Mr. Morrison to say a 
few words. 
4
                 2. B.    04.01.14 
2.       RECOGNITION 
B. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence Mini-Grant Recipients 
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, our 
students and our staff. The Foundation awards grants to help fund projects 
that support the curriculum in every school in the Troy S chool District. This 
year, the Foundation was able to fund  77 grants for a diverse group of 
projects to help enhance learning for students in every school in the 
District. That kind of support is invaluable. 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and talents 
to raise money and support innovative projects that enrich student 
learning. Again, these projects take place in every school in our District and 
many would not be possible without the resources the Foundation 
provides. 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Rich Maziasz, Treasurer of the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize the most 
recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways they are 
enhancing the education of our Troy School District students. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Gino will roll names of recipients/projects on screen as 
Mr. Maziasz speaks. 
5
                     2. C.     04.01.14 
2.       RECOGNITION 
C.  Michigan Schools & Government Credit Union Cash Grant Awards  
This evening we are pleased to recognize seven teachers who received 
cash grant awards from the Michigan Schools and Government Credit 
Union.   
The grants were awarded to teachers at elementary, middle and high 
school levels, for individual projects that the teachers submitted. They 
represent the ingenuity, creativity and commitment of our teachers to find 
ways to bring the curriculum to life for our students. 
Tina Aleshker, from the Michigan Schools & Government Credit Union , will 
recognize the cash grant recipients.

Philippart,  the  following  Board  members  were  present:   Mrs.  Edmunds,  Mrs.  Fleming, 
Mr. Hauff, Mr. Miletti, and Ms. Underwood.   Mr. Schmidt was absent. Superintendent 
Fowler was in attendance as well as administrators Asaro, Keeling, Machesky, Mull, and 
Rajter.  There were no members of the audience.

               6. A.   04.01.14 
6. A.  PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  TEA CONTRACT RATIFICATION  
A tentative agreement between representatives of the Troy Board of Education 
and the Troy Education Association/MEA/NEA (TEA) was reached on March 22, 
2014.  The TEA membership voted to ratify the agreement on March 26, 2014.   
The tentative agreement is a contract for the period of April 1, 2014, through 
June 30, 2015.   The tentative agreement provides all TSD teachers and ancillary 
staff with an off -schedule payment for the 2013/2014 school year in the amount 
of $3,250.  As of July 1, 2014, TSD teachers and ancillary staff employed in such 
positions before the 2013/2014 school year who are eligible for step advancement 
will advance two steps from the 2013/2014 sal ary schedule for placement on a 
new 14-step salary schedule (unless additional step advancement is required to 
ensure that a TSD teacher’s or ancillary staff member’s base salary is not the 
same or lower than it was during the 2012/2013 school year.)   Longevity 
payments for TSD teachers and ancillary staff will remain unchanged and lane 
changes for eligible teachers and ancillary staff will only be made for the 
2014/2015 school year.   All TSD teachers and ancillary staff will also receive a 
Board-paid Tax-Sheltered Annuity contribution during the 2014/2015 school year.  
During the 2014/2015 school year, the number of student instructional days will 
be increased from 179 days to 181 days and the number of teacher work days 
will be increased from 184 days to 1 87 days.   Finally, the tentative agreement 
includes a fund balance provision which will help to maintain the District’s General 
Fund Equity Balance at a minimum of 12% after conclusion of the financial audit 
for the 2014/2015 budget year and which would sh are any additional General 
Fund Equity Balance in excess of 17% after conclusion of the financial audit for 
the 2014/2015 budget year with TSD teachers and ancillary staff. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: 
 Supported by: 
92
 BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education hereby ratifies and 
adopts the tentative agreement reached on March 2 2, 2014  between 
representatives of the Troy Board of Education and the Troy Education 
Association/MEA/NEA for the period of April 1, 2014 through June 30, 2015.

B.  CONSIDERATION:  CONTRACT APPROVAL FOR 
TRANSPORTATION SERVICES 
Invitations to bid were sent to 7 potential bidders with 2 responding 
(none of which were “No Bid”).  The responses were reviewed by Mr. 
Dominick Asaro, Transportation Supervisor. Below is a resolution for 
board consideration regarding transportation services.  
. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education authorize 
administration to proceed with finalizing the contract with First Student to 
provide transportation services for the 201 4/2015, 2015/2016  and 
2016/2017 school years. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
124
Memorandum 
 DATE: March 12, 2014 
Mark Rajter 
 TO:  Dr. Barbara A. Fowler, Superintendent 
Assistant 
Superintendent 
 FROM: Mark S. Rajter, Business Services 
Business 
Services 
 RE: Transportation Services bid award 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-4022,  
Fax - 248/823-4013 
The district sought request for proposals from transportation providers for Pupil 
Transportation Services for the next five fiscal years. RFP 9769 closed on February 11, 
2014 with two providers responding, our current provider, First Student, Inc. and the 
second being Durham School Services. Both provided through comprehensive 
responses. 
Durham’s response was based on an hourly rate and a 4 hour run time. First Student 
prices their service on a per run fee, which is up to 5.5 hours. Therefore, Dominick 
Asaro created a spreadsheet to convert Durham’s pricing into a 5.5 hour run time, to 
compare the two firm’s responses and our current run configurations. 
Attached is the original RFP tabulation sheet and Dominick’s spreadsheet, which 
converts Durham’s proposal into a like-kind per route fee. The annual cost difference is 
$257,527.30, with First Student, Inc. being the lower of the two responses. 
Dominick fully analyzed both proposals’ for price and services to be provided. Based on 
the price differential of $257,000 along with the fact that First Student included the 
impact of the Affordable Care Act onto their price response and their current and past 
performance for the district has met our needs and expectations, Dominick 
recommends Troy continue its relationship with First Student, Inc. for transportation 
services.  
Furthermore, based on my review of the RFP data with Dominick and yourself of each 
of the firm’s proposal, I recommend to you that the Board of Education award to First 
Student, Inc. transportation services for the Troy School District for the next five years 
(initial three years, with up to two years renewal) contingent upon the Superintendent or 
her designee to successfully execute a contract before July 1, 2014.

    RULES AND REGULATIONS    C‐1400 
C‐1402  SMOKINGTOBACCO‐FREE SCHOOLS 
It is the policy of theThe Troy School District that prohibits the use 
and/or  sale  of  any  any  type  of  tobacco  products  and  non‐tobacco 
nicotine  products  (including  smokeless  tobacco  and  any  new 
emerging  tobacco  products)  is  prohibited  in  vehicles,  buildings, 
facilities, structures, or other real estate owned, leased or otherwise 
controlled  by  the  Troy  School  District  and  at  all  school‐sponsored 
events and functions on or off campus, by any person, at any time, 
in  any  location,  and  at  any  event  in  vehicles,  buildings,  facilities, 
structures,  or  other  real  estate  owned,  leased  or  otherwise 
controlled by the Troy School District at all times (24 hours a day – 
7 days a week.) 
For  the  purpose  of  this  policy,  the  use  and/or  sale  of  a  tobacco  or 
non‐tobacco nicotine product is defined as follows: 
 “Tobacco  product”  means  a  preparation  of  tobacco  to  be 
inhaled,  chewed,  or  placed  in  a  person’s  mouth.Tobacco 
products  include,  but  are  not  limited  to,  cigarettes,  cigars,  spit 
tobacco,  shisha  or  hookah  use,  snus,  tobacco  strips,  sticks,  and 
dissolvable  tobacco  products.   Non‐tobacco  nicotine  products 
include,  but  are  not  limited  to,  e‐cigarettes.   This  does  not 
include  products  that  are  classified  as  “nicotine  replacement 
therapy”  and  prescribed  by  a  physician  to  help  tobacco  users 
quit. 
 “Any  person”  includes  students,  employees,  visitors, 
contractors,  delivery  drivers,  etc.,  all  groups  using  school 
property, and any other persons.   
128
 “Any time” means 24 hours per day, seven days per week, and 
365 days per year. 
 “Any  location”  includes  the  school’s  property,  grounds, 
buildings,  and  vehicles,  even  when  school  is  out  of  session  or 
the event is sponsored by another organization. 
 “Any  event”  includes  all  school‐sponsored  events,  whether  on 
or off school property. 
“Use  of  a  tobacco  or  non‐tobacco  nicotine  product”  means  any  of 
the following: 
 The  carrying  by  a  person  of  a  lighted  cigar,  cigarette,  pipe,  or 
  other lighted smoking device. 
 The inhaling or chewing of a tobacco product. 
 The placing of a tobacco product within a person’s mouth. 
Adopted:  February 2, 1999 
Revised:  September, 2003 
  April 20, 2010 
  February 12, 2014

GENERAL SECTION     G-1000 
G-1005 NONDISCRIMINATION 
 In compliance with Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, Title IX of 
the Education Amendments of 1972, Section 504 of the Rehabilitation 
Act of 1973, the Age Discrimination Act of 1975, and the Americans 
with Disabilities Act of 1990, it is the policy of the Troy School 
District that no person shall, on the basis of perceived religion, race, 
color, national origin, age, sex, sexual orientation, disability, height, 
weight, gender identity, marital status, socioeconomic status, or any 
other distinguishing characteristic or association with another person 
who has or is perceived to have any of these characteristics,of race, 
color, religion, national origin or ancestry, gender, age, disability, 
height, weight, or marital status be illegally excluded from 
participation in, be denied the benefits of, or be subjected to 
discrimination during any program, activity, or service or in 
employment. 
 In accordance with Federal regulations, Troy School District has 
appointed the Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources, as the 
Title VI, Title IX, Section 504/ADA, and Age Discrimination Act 
Coordinator.  Any questions, suggestions, or complaints regarding a 
possible or perceived violation of this policy of nondiscrimination 
should be directed to this administrator, who is responsible for 
administering the nondiscrimination grievance procedure: 
Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources 
Title VI, Title IX, Section 504/ADA and Age 
Discrimination Act Coordinator 
Troy School District 
4400 Livernois Road 
Troy, Michigan 48098-4799 
Voice 248.823.4000 
Adopted: July 10, 2007

RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES, CODE OF STUDENT CONDUCT    J‐2000 
J‐2012  ANTI‐BULLYING POLICY 
It is the policy of the District to provide a safe educational environment for all 
students and employees.  Bullying of a student or employee at school, in any 
building within the Troy School District boundaries or any TSD event is strictly 
prohibited. This policy shall be interpreted and enforced to protect all students 
and employees to equally prohibit bullying without regard to its subject matter 
or motivating animus.   
A. Prohibited Conduct.  
1. Bullying.  Bullying of a student or employee at school is strictly prohibited.  
For the purposes of this policy, ʺbullyingʺ shall be defined as: 
Any written, verbal, or physical act, or any electronic communication, that is 
intended or that a reasonable person would know is likely to harm one or more 
students or employees either directly or indirectly by doing any of the 
following:   
a.  Substantially interfering with educational opportunities, benefits, or 
programs of one or more students or employees;  
b.  Adversely affecting a studentʹs or employee’s ability to participate in or 
benefit from  
the Districtʹs educational programs or activities by placing the student or 
employee in reasonable fear of physical harm or by causing substantial 
emotional distress;  
c.  Having an actual and substantial detrimental effect on a student or 
employeeʹs   
  physical or mental health; or 
d.  Causing substantial disruption in, or substantial interference with, the  
  orderly operation of the school.   
2.  Retaliation/False Accusation.  Retaliation or false accusation against a target of 
bullying, anyone reporting bullying, a witness, or another person with reliable 
information about an act of bullying is strictly prohibited.   
131
B.  Reporting an Incident.  If a student, staff member, or other individual believes 
there has been an incident of bullying in violation of this policy, s/he shall promptly 
report such incident to the appropriate principal or designee,  
 C.  Investigation.   All reported allegations of a policy violation or related 
complaint about bullying shall be promptly and thoroughly investigated by the 
building principal or designee.  Each verified incident of bullying and resulting 
consequences including discipline and referrals will be reported annually to the 
school board. 
D.  Notice to Parent/Guardian.  If the principal or designee determines that an 
incident of bullying has occurred, s/he shall promptly provide notification of same to 
the parent/guardian of the victim of the bullying and the parent/guardian of the 
perpetrator of the bullying.   
E.  Annual Reports.  At least annually, the building principal or designee, or the 
Responsible School Official shall report all verified incidents of bullying and the 
resulting consequences, including any disciplinary action or referrals, to the Board of 
Education.   
F.  Responsible School Official.   The superintendent shall be responsible for 
ensuring the proper implementation of this policy throughout the District.  The 
foregoing appointment shall not reduce or eliminate the duties and responsibilities of 
a principal or designee as described in this policy. 
G.  Posting/Publication of Policy.  Notice of this policy will be: (a) annually 
circulated to all students and staff, (b) posted in conspicuous locations in all school 
buildings and departments within the District, (c) annually discussed with students 
and employees, and (d) incorporated into the teacher, student, and parent/guardian 
handbooks. 
H.  Definitions 
1. ʺ At schoolʺ means in a classroom, elsewhere on school premises, on a school 
bus or other school‐related vehicle, or at a school‐sponsored activity or event 
whether or not it is held on school premises.  ʺAt schoolʺ also includes any 
conduct using a telecommunications access device or telecommunications service 
provider that occurs off school premises if the device or provider is owned by or 
under the Districtʹs control. 
2. ʺ Telecommunications Access Deviceʺ means that term as defined in Section 
219a of the Michigan Penal Code, 1931 PA 328, MCL 750.219a, as may be 
amended from time to time.

3. Operations & Maintenance
Implement New Work Order System
HISTORY
Work order system maintenance department was using was outdated and a non‐
integrated system, replacement was sought:
District moved to utilizing SchoolDude software in 2013
 The software is used by more than 6,000 educational institutions across the U.S.
 First ‐ Facility Usage solution implemented in 2013
o Allows stakeholders to efficiently book facility space as needed
o Facilitates inside/outside event request online
 Second ‐ Maintenance solution implemented during FY 2013/14
o Phase 1 – General tasks have been defined in the system
◊ TSD maintenance staff and custodial building leads have received user training
◊ Work orders will be solely placed into SchoolDude starting week of April 7‐11, 2014
o Phase 2 –D e p l o y iPads to maintenance techs for in‐field utilization/reporting
o Phase 3 –D e v e l o p reports to be utilized by management
o Future goal –H a v e contracted staff utilize for general repairs, minor P. M . 
services, etc. tentatively fall of 2014

Human Resources
Operational Goals
1. Established regular Business/Human Resources Department
meetings to review existing systems and processes and to
immediately respond to concerns in a timely manner .
2. Involvement, support, and assistance with the transition to an
updated Finance/Human Resources management system.
3. On ‐going development of links and resources for access by TSD
employees on the TSD website and Intranet. (Ex. Explanation of
“Common” Payroll Deduction Codes, TEAM Evaluation
Resources, New Policies and Administrative Procedures, etc.)
142
Human Resources
Validate impact of newly implemented 
hiring process.
1. On ‐going dialogue and work with Principals and Assistant
Superintendent’s of Instruction to enhance our hiring process through
the addition/modification of the performance‐based aspects of our hiring
processes for teachers and administrators.
2. On ‐going dialogue with Principals about the successes and challenges of
our most recent hires and additional supports for our newest teachers.
3. Future plans to analyze the 3 ‐year performance evaluation outcomes
under the Troy Educator Appraisal Model (TEAM) for all TSD probationary
teachers to validate the impact of the enhanced hiring process and to
consider modifications.
143
Human Resources
Continue to expand initiatives to employ a 
highly qualified diverse workforce.
1. Continue to emphasize the importance of a diverse workforce
within the hiring process for all TSD positions.
2. Exploring the utilization of proven structured interviews for special
education teachers, ancillary staff, and para‐educator applicants.
3. Considering teacher recruiting venues outside of Michigan’s public
universities which would provide access to a more culturally ‐
diverse teacher workforce.
4. Meeting with community members participating on the TSD
Diversity & Inclusion Council to consider broadening our network
and job posting locations.

• We will continue to monitor intervention and acceleration opportunities for students
‐ Investigate NWEA testing model 
• Committee comprised of 8 principals representing all 3 levels, Curriculum Coordinators, and 
Dr. Machesky and Mrs. Keeling investigated both NWEA and ACT Aspire products
• Met with representatives from NWEA and ACT to learn about their products
• Committee visited or met with districts currently using either NWEA or ACT
• Committee recommended that we continue to administer the ACT in 11th grade and that we  administer
the ACT Aspire product that replaces the EXPLORE and PLAN in grades 8 and 10.  Without definitive   
information from the state about its assessment plan for students in grades 3‐11, we do not feel it is 
advisable at this time to initiate any additional testing.
‐ Expand implementation of teacher labs
• All 4th & 5th grade teachers participated in Teacher Labs focused on ELA
• The TLLs piloted Teacher Lab with 12 teachers focused on Math
• Ellen Cale piloted Teacher Lab with 12 teachers focused on Visible Thinking

Continue interventions to raise the achievement 
level of lowest performing subgroups.
Math
•Creation of math support class providing an additional 3 hours of instruction per week for middle
school students who need it most;
•Assured students with IEPs have opportunities to learn at the level of the Common Core State
Standards;
•Materials adoption review included expectations for equity and differentiation;
•Continued support for high school Algebra 1 intervention programs.
English Language Arts
•Professional learning for teachers and paraprofessionals in literacy acquisition for English 
language learners;
•Aligned teaching and learning in basic high school courses at grade level with Common Core
State Standards;
•Coordinated professional development and support for reading teachers at the elementary and
middle school level.
Science and Social Studies
•Professional learning in disciplinary literacy strategies for content area teachers transitioning 
students into the critical thinking, reading, and writing required for academic work;
•Improved  instruction for all students.
